| Verson 1 |  |  |
| Weeks | Topics | Deliverables  |
| Week 1: | Intro to the Final Project Phase + Our Approach to Leadership Development | Goal-setting paper  |
| Week 2: | Intro to 1-1 Inquiry (to build a solution as a software engineer / AI engineer / data scientist) | Weekly self-reflection  |
| Week 3: | In-class Practice in 1-1 Coaching | Weekly self-reflection + Peer feedback |
| Week 4: | Coaching a Professional from a company/community for Problem Inquiry | 1-1 Coaching  recording |
| Week 5:  | Intro to Team Facilitation for Further Problem Inquiry | Weekly self-reflection |
| Week 6: | In-class Practice in Team Facilitation | Weekly self-reflection + Peer feedback  |
| Week 7: | Facilitate a group of Professionals from the same company/community for further inquiry | Team facilitation recording  |
| Week 8: | Introduction to the Organisational Leadership | Weekly self-reflection  |
| Week 9:  | In-class Practice in Organisational Leadership (Sts present their project proposal in class + get feedback) | Weekly self-reflection + Proposal submission + Peer feedback  |
| Week 10:  | Presenting your Project Proposal in the organisation  | Project proposal + project plan + software requirements specs + software design specs  |

|  |  |  |
| Workshops | Learn about and practice coaching, facilitation and organisational leadership |  |
| Group Supervision  | Get support from your supervision on your project idea, proposal, planning  |  |
| Squads | Get peer support on your project, leadership challenges etc. |  |
| Standups  | Share weekly progress with peers |  |

|  | understand and build a solution  |  |

| 1\. one-to-one level | understand their needs / goals  | need a process guideline |
|  | make them aware of the possible solutions  |  |
|  | get their commitment  |  |
| 2\. group level  | team workshop & use a group dynamic to come up with a solution together that will turn into a software - voices are heard. | need a process guideline |
| 3\. organisational level  | organisational level - put everyting in a summary and propose a solution | need a process guideline  |

| Foundational Competencies | qwasar skills | \[merged\]  | Technical Competencies | qwasar skills | \[merged\]  | Leadership Competencies | qwasar skills | \[merged\]  |
| mathematical thinking | breaking down tasks into smaller components, identifying similar tasks that map help, identifying appropriate knowledge and skills, identifying assumptioons, selecting an approptiate strategy, considering alternative approaches, looking for patterns or connections, generating examples | \[merged\]  | Programming  | Coding basics and fundamentals, variables, loop statements, the coding environment, the terminal, imperative programming, coding norms, scripting, basic Unix programming, event programming, declarative programming, Bash/Shell Elixir and functional programming, expert C++, imperative programming, Rigor Writing readable, norm-conforming, and maintainable code  back-end programming fundamental understanding of how computers work, handle and display characters of all sorts and tyles; fundamental understanding of data structures, understanding of programming languages' fundamental make-up and syntax  front-end programming comprehension of common front-end programming languages, tools, and frameworks; ability to use front-end capabilities to create websites, applications, and mobile apps that function, create a good UX, and have structured style and display guidelines   Programming languages C, C++, Elixir, CSS/HTML, React, Javascript, some Java, Ruby, Python, or Ruby on Rails | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
| scientific thinking | set of reasoning processes including induction, deduction, experimental design, causal reasoning, concept formation, hypothesis testing, and more; | \[merged\]  | Analysing Software requirements | Ability to translate technical specs into successful architecture for larger and more complex projects | \[merged\]  | Employability | Technical interview preparation and practice (structured problem solving and simultaneous communication), presentation of self on paper, online, and in interviews | \[merged\]  |
| logic | the ability to identify structure, reasoning, relationships (or lack of) among facts, and seeing things in a particular order, identifying skips or jumps thus uncovering assumptions | \[merged\]  | Software design and architecture | object-oriented design,  basic architecture, software architecture, web application architecture, deep software architecture, rebuilding video conferencing application,rebuilding Slack, FTP and network programming, protocols, servers (?), Strong architecture knowledge | \[merged\]  | Self-awareness and management | Management of self under stress and deadlines, knowledge of responses to emotions, critique, and feedback | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | software development and Software Development Cycle | Comprehension of and experience with going from 0 too a completed and successfully deployed and functioning piece of software including the ability to translate software requirements and technical specifications into an architecture, code, designs, and working software. | \[merged\]  | Problem solving | Structured approach to solving problems, ability to identify problem, ability to break down problem into smaller elements, ability to see big picture and achieve small steps | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | software testing | Unix low-level debugging, Testing and debugging | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | software deployment | Deployment of software to production including cloud-based deployments Servers, Kubernetes (automated deployment) | \[merged\]  | Team work | Successful coordination with others, contributes to others’ or common code, proactively contributes to team and problem solving | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | software maintanance  | reading, reviewing, and adding to code written by another programmer; writing maintainable code | \[merged\]  | Communication | Ability to communicate on technical requirements, code review and feedback, your role and others’ roles, your work and others’ work, project requirements, program commitments, disappointment in self and in teammates, ability to express opinions and feelings under control and with respect, asking for help from others | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Database management | databases (SQL and noSQL), advanced databases,  | \[merged\]  | Perseverance | Not giving up until problem is solved or code is working; diligence; | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Data structures and algorithms | basic algorithms and use of functions and formulas in coding, advanced comprehension and use of data structures, comprehension and use of common algorithms used in AI and cloud-based AI tools, common databases and frameworks used, SQL databases | \[merged\]  | Mentoring | Mentors younger learners, helps them to learn and develop as a programmer, fosters problem-solving competencies and encourages learning and autonomy. | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Software security | SSL, RSA, using APIs | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Software for Hardware | memory management, memory allocation, Kernel | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Maintaining code quality | Write readable and easy-to-maintain code, uses best practices in code structure and creation, writes clean code with little guidance, consistently follows best practices | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
|  |  | \[merged\]  | Technical knowledge | Broad knowledge of software engineering fundamentals, principles, languages, tools, frameworks, industry standards and best practices, and resources; knowledge of one high level language and one low level language | \[merged\]  |  |  | \[merged\]  |
